Appendix H. Complete fresh mathematical replay runner
The following full file is part of the proof and audit cost.
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Fresh exhaustive kernel census and complete-presentation recognition.
Inputs are exactly three printed GAP/data programs; no saved census, private
primary text, Atlas permutation array or SmallGroups catalogue is read.
The output directory must not exist. GAP/NQ remain trusted exact algorithms.
"""
import argparse
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path
import re
import shutil
import subprocess
import time
ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
INPUTS = ["phase78_n009_integration_census.g", "phase78_n009_integration_finite.g",
"phase78_n009_integration_data.g"]
CASES = [(3, 3, 61, 37, 3, 20, 3904, [ 7, 23, 61]), ( 3, 5, 401, 235, 15, 125, 25664, [ 7, 23, 61,
147, 401]), ( 4, 3, 1483, 199, 11, 105, 379648, [ 31, 267, 1483]), ( 5, 1, 127, 15, 1, 8, 130048,
[127])]
FINAL = "PASS complete_presentations=214 actual_IP_meets=43840 atlas=72 raw_examples=34
exceptional=2 finite_universals=106 exact_census_facets=428 table6_rows=107 table5_rows=12"
def require(condition, message):
if not condition:
raise RuntimeError(message)
def sha(path):
return hashlib.sha256(path.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
def main():
ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description= __doc__)
ap.add_argument("--gap", default = "gap")
ap.add_argument("--output-dir", type= Path, required = True)
ap.add_argument("--timeout", type= int, default = 1200)
args = ap.parse_args()
out = args.output_dir.resolve()
require(not out.exists(), "Refusing existing output directory" )
bindings = [{"path": n, "sha256": sha(ROOT / n)} for n in INPUTS]
out.mkdir(parents= True)
start = time.monotonic()
for n in INPUTS:
shutil.copy2(ROOT / n, out / n)
timings = {}
for name, program in [("census", INPUTS[ 0]), ( "finite", INPUTS[ 1])]:
t = time.monotonic()
run = subprocess.run([args.gap, "-q", "-T", program], cwd = out, text = True,
stdout= subprocess.PIPE, stderr = subprocess.PIPE, timeout = args.timeout, check = False)
(out / (name + ".log")).write_text(run.stdout)
(out / (name + ".err")).write_text(run.stderr)
require(run.returncode == 0 and not run.stderr.strip(), f"{name}: execution error or
warning")
require(not re.search(r"(^|\n)(Error,|Syntax error: |brk>)", run.stdout), f"{name}: GAP
failure")
timings[name] = round(time.monotonic() - t, 6)
census = (out / "census.log").read_text()
finite = (out / "finite.log").read_text()
require(census.count("PASS: complete invariant-kernel ladder" ) == 4 and FINAL in finite,
"Incomplete terminal markers" )
observed = re.findall(r"RESULT rank= (\d+) excess= (\d+) candidates= (\d+) ordered= (\d+)
selfdual= (\d+) up_to_reversal= (\d+) actual_meets= (\d+)", census)
require([tuple(map(int, r)) for r in observed] == [x[:7] for x in CASES], "Census totals
differ")
for d, k, candidates, ordered, fixed, classes, meets, levels in CASES:
for j, n in enumerate(levels, 1):
data = json.loads((out / f"phase78_d{d}_k{k}.level{j}.json").read_text())
require(len(data) == n and len({tuple(x) for x in data}) == n, "Ladder cardinality
or duplicate key" )
accepted = json.loads((out / f"phase78_d{d}_k{k}.accepted.json").read_text())
require(len(accepted) == ordered and sum(x[4] for x in accepted) == fixed, "Accepted
cardinality/fixed count" )
seeds = json.loads((out / "phase78_d3_k3.seed.json").read_text())
require(len(seeds) == 4 and sorted(x[1] for x in seeds) == [1, 2, 3, 4], "All four distinct
seed rows" )
read = lambda suffix: json.loads((out / ("phase78_n009_integration_" + suffix +
".json")).read_text())
require(len(read("all_presentations")) == 214, "Complete presentation count" )
require(read("table4_literal_matches") == [[1, 1], [ 2, 2], [ 3, 3], [ 4, 4], [ 5, 3], [ 6, 1],
[7, 7], [ 8, 3]], "Literal correction map" )
require(len(read("table3_matches")) == 12, "Table3 cells" )
require(len(read("facet_matches")) == 214 and len(read("table6_results")) == 107,
"Facet/table6 rows" )
require(len(read("universal_orders")) == 106, "Finite universals" )
require(len(read("census_matches")) == 8 and sorted(x[1] for x in read("census_matches"))
== list(range(1, 9)), "Eight named source targets" )
versions = re.findall(r"VERSION GAP= (\S+) NQ= (\S+)", census)
require(len(versions) == 4 and len(set(versions)) == 1, "Software identity" )
for row in bindings:
require(sha(ROOT / row["path"]) == row["sha256"] == sha(out / row["path"]),
"Mathematical input changed during replay" )
files = [{"path": p.name, "sha256": sha(p)} for p in sorted(out.iterdir()) if p.is_file()]
report = dict(status= "PASS", software = dict(zip(["GAP", "NQ"], versions[ 0])),
elapsed_seconds= round(time.monotonic()-start, 6), timings = timings,
cases= [dict(zip(["rank", "excess", "terminal_kernels", "ordered", "selfdual",
"classes", "actual_meets", "ladder_sizes"], c)) for c in CASES],
total_census_meets= 539264, complete_presentations = 214, presentation_IP_meets = 43840,
source_table6_rows= 107, source_table5_rows = 12, named_census_facet_isomorphisms = 428,
source_statement_scope= "Corrected principal contract with explicitly retained
geometric/square/infinite inputs; no historical novelty or Lean verification." ,
candidate_generation_uses_source_totals= False, SmallGroups_dependency = False,
old_census_input= False, private_primary_input = False,
inputs= bindings, runner = dict(path= Path(__file__).name, sha256 = sha(Path(__file__))),
outputs= files)
(out / "phase78_n009_integration_run.json").write_text(json.dumps(report, indent = 2)+"\n")
print(json.dumps({k: report[k] for k in ["status", "software", "elapsed_seconds",
"total_census_meets", "complete_presentations", "presentation_IP_meets"]}))
if __name__ == "__main__":
main()
References and exact source versions
[BC98] F. Buekenhout and P. Cara, Some properties of inductively minimal geometries , Bulletin of the Belgian Math-
ematical Society–Simon Stevin 5 (1998), 213–219, DOI 10.36045/bbms/1103409005. Target version: complete au-
thored text rendered at the author-uploaded Academia entry , locally recorded with displayed lines 181–220. No PDF-
version identity is inferred.
[CC02] P. J. Cameron and P. Cara, Independent generating sets and geometries for symmetric groups, Journal of Alge-
bra 258 (2002), 641–650, DOI 10.1016/S0021-8693(02)00550-1. Target version: the twelve-page author manuscript;
result page references in the ledger refer to that manuscript rather than the journal pagination.
[BCD97] F. Buekenhout, P. Cara and M. Dehon, Inductively minimal flag-transitive geometries, in N. L. Johnson (ed.),
Mostly Finite Geometries, Marcel Dekker, 1997, 185–190. Retained in the precise form restated in [BC98], Lemma 1
and its following paragraph; the original 1997 chapter was not independently acquired in this assembly.
[W00] J. Whiston, Maximal independent generating sets of the symmetric group , Journal of Algebra 232 (2000), 255–
268. W1 and W2 are retained in the forms recited in [CC02], pages 3–4; the original proof and its classification inputs
are not reproved or newly source-audited here.
[Coset] The (G1)–(G4) conventions and general coset correspondence are those stated in [CC02], pages 7–8, with its
references to F. Buekenhout, Foundations of incidence geometry, in Handbook of Incidence Geometry (1995), 63–105;
F. Buekenhout and M. Hermand, On flag-transitive geometries and groups (1991), 45–78; and J. Tits, Buildings of
Spherical Type and Finite BN-Pairs (1974).

Operations and covers
1. M. I. Hartley and D. Leemans, On the thin regular geometries of rank four for the Janko group J1 , Innovations
in Incidence Geometry 1 (2005), 181–190. Journal primary PDF . Main construction targets: Theorems 3.2, 4.2,
5.2, 5.4, 6.2, 7.2. Its introductory exhaustive classification is recovered in Corollary O6.3.
2. A. Montero and A. Ivić Weiss, Locally Spherical Hypertopes from Generalised Cubes , arXiv:2001.11082v1, 29
January 2020. Targets: Theorem 3.1, Proposition 3.2, Corollary 3.3, and the principal applications in §4. Later
journal numbering is not substituted.
3. C. A. Piedade and P. Tranchida,Constructing new geometries: a generalized approach to halving for hypertopes,
arXiv:2405.19050v1, especially its introduction and §2.3 on lattice nondegeneracy, its graph constructions, and
the established bipartiteness/index-two interpretation. This is attribution and terminology support, not an addi-
tional compressed manuscript or an import of its broader geometric conclusion without (PC).
4. L. Danzer, Regular incidence-complexes and dimensionally unbounded sequences of such. I*, North-Holland
Mathematics Studies 87 (1984), 115–127; P . McMullen and E. Schulte, Abstract Regular Polytopes* (2002),
especially the generalized-cube construction and standard coordinate models. The construction theorem in (O9)
and standard convex-polytope orders remain retained; the particular toroidal lattice identifications are proved in
§9.
5. The classical finite/affine/compact-hyperbolic Coxeter classifications, and the GAP 4.15.1 exact permutation-
group/coset algorithms with TomLib 1.2.11’s complete J1 subgroup catalogue and permutation model, are the
other substantial inputs. The execution manifest binds the actual installed versions and data.

ory, Series A 204 (2024), 105839, DOI 10.1016/j.jcta.2023.105839. Target: author manuscript headed 25 July 2023 ,
eight pages; publisher full text unacquired.

878. The five rows and embedded chirality input retained in §22.3 were checked in arXiv:2009.04566, Proposition 4.2

[Ens18] E. Ens, Rank 4 toroidal hypertopes , Ars Mathematica Contemporanea 15 (2018), 67–79. The selected defini-
tions, equations (3.1), (4.1) and Theorem 4.3 were read to match the lattice convention in §9; the full classification is
neither assumed to prove that quotient nor counted.
[BBL01] D. Bonheure, F. Buekenhout and D. Leemans, On the Petrials of thin rank 3 geometries, Journal of Geometry
71 (2001), 19–25, DOI 10.1007/s00022-001-8548-2. Target version: complete seven-page authored rendering hosted
at the coauthor-linked Academia article; primary PDF unavailable. The source operation and targets are attributed to
this work. Its unconditional embedding and length/order assertions are corrected in §23, without a historical-priority
claim.
The new retained-object interface K(c) and its coset/endpoint/Petrial applications are explicit integration arguments;
no claim of historical originality is made for the elementary ingredients. The two-branch corrected Petrial proof was
developed and independently reviewed in the earlier phase25 component and is reproved here using the common inter-
face. The phase45 reference erratum is incorporated in this new manuscript: the citation after O4.1 is to N013’s quoted
Theorem 2.2. No frozen predecessor is altered.

Its quoted standard intersection criteria are attributed to M. Conder and D. Oliveros, The intersection condition for
regular polytopes , JCTA 120 (2013), 1291–1304. The universal and finite square-quotient inputs are quoted from
McMullen–Schulte, Abstract Regular Polytopes (2002), the latter on pp. 364–365, 368. Its exceptional infinite de-
composition is quoted from McMullen–Schulte, Regular polytopes from twisted Coxeter groups and unitary reflexion
groups, Advances in Mathematics 82 (1990), 35–87. Those earlier proofs are retained in the precise quoted forms; no
claim of fresh acquisition or rederivation is made.
The named-object data are credited to M. I. Hartley’s Atlas of Small Regular Polytopes; Appendix G records the exact
primary URL and snapshot hash for every defining presentation. The URL may contain a catalogue identifier, but
replay neither calls SmallGroups nor assumes a catalogue is complete. The entire new finite search and recognition
were run with GAP 4.15.1 and NQ 2.5.11. Existing software/background inputs for the other branches are unchanged.
